    \newsavebox\pandoc@box
    \newcommand*\pandocbounded[1]{%
      \sbox\pandoc@box{#1}%
      % scaling factors for width and height
      \Gscale@div\@tempa\textheight{\dimexpr\ht\pandoc@box+\dp\pandoc@box\relax}%
      \Gscale@div\@tempb\linewidth{\wd\pandoc@box}%
      % select the smaller of both
      \ifdim\@tempb\p@<\@tempa\p@
        \let\@tempa\@tempb
      \fi
      % scaling accordingly (\@tempa < 1)
      \ifdim\@tempa\p@<\p@
        \scalebox{\@tempa}{\usebox\pandoc@box}%
      % scaling not needed, use as it is
      \else
        \usebox{\pandoc@box}%
      \fi
    }

\def\PY@reset{\let\PY@it=\relax \let\PY@bf=\relax%
    \let\PY@ul=\relax \let\PY@tc=\relax%
    \let\PY@bc=\relax \let\PY@ff=\relax}
\def\PY@tok#1{\csname PY@tok@#1\endcsname}
\def\PY@toks#1+{\ifx\relax#1\empty\else%

\def\PY@do#1{\PY@bc{\PY@tc{\PY@ul{%
    \PY@it{\PY@bf{\PY@ff{#1}}}}}}}
\def\PY#1#2{\PY@reset\PY@toks#1+\relax+\PY@do{#2}}

    % For linebreaks inside Verbatim environment from package fancyvrb.
    \makeatletter
        \newbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox
        \newbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox
        \newcommand*\Wrappedvisiblespace {\textcolor{red}{\textvisiblespace}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol {\textcolor{red}{\llap{\tiny$\m@th\hookrightarrow$}}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationindent {3ex }
        \newcommand*\Wrappedafterbreak {\kern\Wrappedcontinuationindent\copy\Wrappedcontinuationbox}
        % Take advantage of the already applied Pygments mark-up to insert
        % potential linebreaks for TeX processing.
        %        {, <, #, %, $, ' and ": go to next line.
        %        _, }, ^, &, >, - and ~: stay at end of broken line.
        % Use of \textquotesingle for straight quote.
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatspecials {%
            \def\PYGZus{\discretionary{\char`\_}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\_}}%
            \def\PYGZob{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\{}{\char`\{}}%
            \def\PYGZcb{\discretionary{\char`\}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\}}}%
            \def\PYGZca{\discretionary{\char`\^}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\^}}%
            \def\PYGZam{\discretionary{\char`\&}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\&}}%
            \def\PYGZlt{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\<}{\char`\<}}%
            \def\PYGZgt{\discretionary{\char`\>}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\>}}%
            \def\PYGZsh{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\#}{\char`\#}}%
            \def\PYGZpc{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\%}{\char`\%}}%
            \def\PYGZdl{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\$}{\char`\$}}%
            \def\PYGZhy{\discretionary{\char`\-}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\-}}%
            \def\PYGZsq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\textquotesingle}{\textquotesingle}}%
            \def\PYGZdq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\"}{\char`\"}}%
            \def\PYGZti{\discretionary{\char`\~}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\~}}%
        }
        % Some characters . , ; ? ! / are not pygmentized.
        % This macro makes them "active" and they will insert potential linebreaks
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatpunct {%
            \lccode`\~`\.\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\.}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\.}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\,\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\,}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\,}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\;\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\;}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\;}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\:\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\:}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\:}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\?\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\?}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\?}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\!\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\!}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\!}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\/\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\/}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\/}}}%
            \catcode`\.\active
            \catcode`\,\active
            \catcode`\;\active
            \catcode`\:\active
            \catcode`\?\active
            \catcode`\!\active
            \catcode`\/\active
            \lccode`\~`\~
        }
    \makeatother

    \let\OriginalVerbatim=\Verbatim
    \makeatletter
    \renewcommand{\Verbatim}[1][1]{%
        %\parskip\z@skip
        \sbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox {\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol}%
        \sbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox {\FV@SetupFont\Wrappedvisiblespace}%
        \def\FancyVerbFormatLine ##1{\hsize\linewidth
            \vtop{\raggedright\hyphenpenalty\z@\exhyphenpenalty\z@
                \doublehyphendemerits\z@\finalhyphendemerits\z@
                \strut ##1\strut}%
        }%
        % If the linebreak is at a space, the latter will be displayed as visible
        % space at end of first line, and a continuation symbol starts next line.
        % Stretch/shrink are however usually zero for typewriter font.
        \def\FV@Space {%
            \nobreak\hskip\z@ plus\fontdimen3\font minus\fontdimen4\font
            \discretionary{\copy\Wrappedvisiblespacebox}{\Wrappedafterbreak}
            {\kern\fontdimen2\font}%
        }%

        % Allow breaks at special characters using \PYG... macros.
        \Wrappedbreaksatspecials
        % Breaks at punctuation characters . , ; ? ! and / need catcode=\active
        \OriginalVerbatim[#1,codes*=\Wrappedbreaksatpunct]%
    }
    \makeatother

    \section{Probabilistic Analysis of Graph
Watermarking}\label{probabilistic-analysis-of-graph-watermarking}

\textbf{Probabilistic Machine Learning --- Course Assignment - Yu Zhang
- yuzhang@cs.aau.dk}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\textbf{Research question:} When a dataset watermark is injected into a
graph --- perturbing node features to embed an ownership signal --- how
does this perturbation propagate across three levels of a Graph
Convolutional Network (GCN) pipeline: the \emph{feature space}, the
\emph{embedding space}, and the \emph{prediction space}? And can
probabilistic models quantify each level of impact?

\textbf{Three-level analysis framework:}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.1842}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2895}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5263}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Level
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Observable
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Probabilistic Tool
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Feature & \(\Delta x = x_{\rm wm} - x_{\rm orig}\) & VAE (latent space
deviation) \\
Embedding & \(\Delta H = H_{\rm mark} - H_{\rm benign}\) & GMM (cluster
assignment shift) \\
Prediction & Softmax \(\rightarrow\) posterior predictive & Bayesian
classifier + SVI \\
\end{longtable}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\textbf{Submission note:} The watermarked dataset is generated by a
private research pipeline (not publicly downloadable). This notebook is
submitted as PDF per the assignment's alternative submission option.

\subsection{Section 1 --- Problem Description \&
Dataset}\label{section-1-problem-description-dataset}

\subsubsection{1.1 Background: Graph
Watermarking}\label{background-graph-watermarking}

As graph neural networks (GNNs) are increasingly trained on valuable
curated datasets, \textbf{dataset intellectual property (IP) protection}
becomes critical. Dataset watermarking embeds a hidden ownership signal
directly into the training data, such that any model trained on it
carries a detectable fingerprint --- without any modification to the
training procedure.

In the method studied here, watermarking operates at the \textbf{feature
level}: for a selected subset of nodes \(V_w \subset V\), a small
bounded perturbation is applied to each node's feature vector: \[
x_{\rm wm,v} = x_{\rm orig,v} + \Delta x_v, \quad \|\Delta x_v\|_\infty \leq \delta,
\] where \(\delta = 0.5\) is the perturbation budget. The perturbation
is \textbf{not random} --- it is optimised to produce a structured,
detectable shift in the GCN embedding space of any model trained on the
modified data, while keeping the feature change imperceptible.

\textbf{Why is a probabilistic approach appropriate?}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The perturbation \(\Delta x\) is designed to be imperceptible at the
  feature level --- its statistical signature is subtle. Probabilistic
  models can capture distributional shifts that point estimates miss.
\item
  GCN embeddings are high-dimensional; modeling their distribution
  (rather than point values) via GMMs reveals \emph{structural} changes
  invisible to scalar metrics like L2 distance.
\item
  Node classification under watermarking introduces \emph{uncertainty}:
  a Bayesian classifier provides posterior predictive distributions ---
  not just class labels --- revealing which nodes the model is genuinely
  uncertain about.
\end{itemize}

    \subsubsection{1.3 Watermarked Node
Properties}\label{watermarked-node-properties}

The \textbf{random selection strategy} samples \(V_w\) uniformly at
random within each class, selecting an equal number of nodes per class
(\(\approx 97\) per class across 40 classes). There is \textbf{no
graph-structural constraint} --- the selection is purely random.

Despite this, the watermarked nodes have a mean out-degree of
\(\approx 4.6\), notably lower than the full-graph mean of \(8.5\). This
is not a selection bias but a \textbf{class-balanced sampling effect}:
the WM sample mean reflects the \emph{unweighted} average of per-class
mean degrees (\(\approx 4.4\)), whereas the full-graph mean (\(8.5\)) is
dominated by a few high-degree classes (e.g.~class 16: mean degree
\(14.3\)). Because equal numbers are drawn from every class, high-degree
classes carry no extra weight.

\PY{n}{deg\PYZus{}all} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{degree}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{deg\PYZus{}wm}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{degree}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{deg\PYZus{}non} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{degree}\PY{p}{[}\PY{o}{\PYZti{}}\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} -- Perturbation magnitude ---------------------------------------------------}

\PY{n}{delta\PYZus{}linf} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{delta\PYZus{}x}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{abs}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{max}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{dim}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{values}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} per\PYZhy{}node Linf}
\PY{n}{delta\PYZus{}l2}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{delta\PYZus{}x}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{norm}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{dim}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}               \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} per\PYZhy{}node L2}

\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}np}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{=== Watermarked nodes (V\PYZus{}w) ===}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{1.4 Summary}\label{summary}

\textbf{Key observations from Figure 1:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{(a) Balanced classes:} The subgraph is constructed to equalise
  class sizes (\textasciitilde2,080 nodes/class), removing the long-tail
  bias of the original ogbn-arxiv. This ensures our probabilistic models
  are not dominated by majority classes.
\item
  \textbf{(b) Class-balanced sampling effect on degree:} Random
  selection draws equal numbers from each of the 40 CS sub-field
  classes. Per-class mean out-degrees range from \(1.0\) to \(14.3\);
  the watermarked node mean (\(\approx 4.6\)) therefore reflects the
  \emph{unweighted} average of class-specific means, not the
  population-weighted mean (\(8.5\)). High-degree classes (e.g.~cs.NI)
  receive no extra representation, so the WM degree distribution
  visually sits below the non-WM distribution in the boxplot.
\item
  \textbf{(c) Perturbation concentrated near budget ceiling:} The
  \(\|\Delta x_v\|_\infty\) distribution is strongly right-skewed: 65\%
  of watermarked nodes have perturbation magnitude \(> 0.4\), with mean
  \(\approx 0.40\). The optimiser actively exploits most of the
  available budget, constrained only by the regularisation terms
  (\(\lambda_1 = \lambda_2 = 0.1\)).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Research roadmap:} The three sections that follow apply distinct
probabilistic models to each analysis level: 1. \textbf{Section 3
(VAE):} Does the optimised \(\Delta x\) push watermarked nodes out of
the normal feature distribution? 2. \textbf{Section 4 (GMM):} Does the
embedding shift \(\Delta H\) change cluster membership in GCN
representation space? 3. \textbf{Section 5 (Bayesian Classifier + SVI):}
Does watermarking increase prediction uncertainty for the affected
nodes?

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Section 2 --- Exploratory Data
Analysis}\label{section-2-exploratory-data-analysis}

We examine the dataset across three complementary lenses: raw feature
statistics, the structure of the perturbation \(\Delta x\), and the
geometry of the feature space via PCA. Together these answer: \emph{what
does the watermark look like, and where does it live?}

    \subsubsection{2.1 Feature Statistics}\label{feature-statistics}

    \subsubsection{2.3 PCA Visualization of Feature
Space}\label{pca-visualization-of-feature-space}

Principal Component Analysis (PCA) projects the 128-dimensional feature
vectors onto a 2D plane that maximises explained variance. This lets us
visualise the \textbf{geometry} of the feature space and observe whether
the watermark perturbation \(\Delta x\) is visible as a systematic
shift.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{7}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} -- PCA of x\PYZus{}orig ------------------------------------------------------------}
\PY{k+kn}{from}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nn}{sklearn}\PY{n+nn}{.}\PY{n+nn}{decomposition}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kn}{import} \PY{n}{PCA}

\PY{n}{pca} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{PCA}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{n\PYZus{}components}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{random\PYZus{}state}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{SEED}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{X2d\PYZus{}orig} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{pca}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{fit\PYZus{}transform}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}orig}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{detach}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (N, 2)}

\PY{n}{var\PYZus{}exp}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{pca}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{explained\PYZus{}variance\PYZus{}ratio\PYZus{}}

    \subsubsection{2.4 EDA Summary}\label{eda-summary}

\textbf{Figure 2 key observations:} - \textbf{(a)} The \(x_{\rm wm}\)
distribution for WM nodes overlaps heavily with \(x_{\rm orig}\),
confirming imperceptibility at first glance --- but a slight shift in
the tails is visible. - \textbf{(b)} The perturbation is \emph{not}
spread uniformly across dimensions; a subset of dimensions absorbs
disproportionately large \(|\Delta x_i|\), suggesting the optimiser
exploits directions of low reconstruction cost. - \textbf{(c)} There is
a \textbf{weak negative trend} between \(\|\Delta x_v\|_2\) and
out-degree --- consistent with the embedding plasticity principle, even
under random node selection. - \textbf{(d)} Per-class perturbation
magnitudes are broadly similar, with some classes showing higher
variance, likely reflecting class-specific feature compressibility.

\textbf{Figure 3 key observations:} - The 40 CS sub-fields form
overlapping clusters in PCA space, not cleanly separated, reflecting the
challenge of the classification task. - WM nodes (black in panel a) are
scattered throughout all clusters --- no PCA-visible concentration ---
confirming the random selection strategy. - Panel (b) shows the
PCA-space shift \(\Delta x\): arrows are short and point in diverse
directions, making the watermark \textbf{visually indistinguishable}
from natural feature variation.

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Section 3 --- Probabilistic Model
Design}\label{section-3-probabilistic-model-design}

We deploy \textbf{three probabilistic models}, one for each analysis
level:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}

  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2188}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2188}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3438}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Level
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Input
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Model
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright

\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Feature (\(x\)) & \(x_{\rm orig}\), \(x_{\rm wm}\) & \textbf{VAE} & SVI
(Pyro) \\
Embedding (\(\tilde{H}\)) & \(\tilde{A}^2 x\) & \textbf{GMM} & EM
(sklearn) \\
Prediction (\(\hat{y}\)) & \(\tilde{A}^2 x\) & \textbf{Bayesian Logistic
Reg.} & SVI (Pyro) \\
\end{longtable}

The graph-smoothed features \(\tilde{H} = \tilde{A}^2 X\) are
pre-computed in S3.1 and shared by the GMM and Bayesian classifier.

    \subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{3.1 Graph-Smoothed Features
\(\tilde{A}^2 X\)}{3.1 Graph-Smoothed Features \textbackslash tilde\{A\}\^{}2 X}}\label{graph-smoothed-features-tildea2-x}

A 2-layer GCN with identity weight matrices performs two rounds of
normalised neighbourhood aggregation: \[
\tilde{H} = \tilde{A}\,\tilde{A}\,X, \quad
\tilde{A} = D^{-1/2}(A+I)D^{-1/2},
\] where \(D\) is the degree matrix of \(A+I\). This is a deterministic
preprocessing step that mixes each node's features with its 2-hop
neighbourhood --- capturing graph structure without any learnable
parameters.

    \subsubsection{3.2 Level 1 --- VAE on Raw
Features}\label{level-1-vae-on-raw-features}

A \textbf{Variational Autoencoder} learns a low-dimensional latent
representation of the \emph{normal} feature distribution \(p(x)\). After
training on \(x_{\rm orig}\), we probe the model with \(x_{\rm wm}\) and
measure how far watermarked features deviate from the learned
distribution.

\textbf{Generative model:} \[
z \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I), \qquad
x \mid z \sim \mathcal{N}(f_\theta(z),\, \sigma^2 I)
\]

\textbf{Variational posterior (guide):} \[
q_\phi(z \mid x) = \mathcal{N}\!\left(\mu_\phi(x),\, \mathrm{diag}(\sigma_\phi(x)^2)\right)
\]

\textbf{ELBO:} \[
\mathcal{L}_{\rm ELBO} = \mathbb{E}_{q_\phi}[\log p_\theta(x|z)]
- D_{\rm KL}\bigl(q_\phi(z|x)\,\|\,p(z)\bigr)
\]

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{9}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} -- VAE definition -----------------------------------------------------------}
\PY{k+kn}{import}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nn}{torch}\PY{n+nn}{.}\PY{n+nn}{nn}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{as}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nn}{nn}

\PY{n}{Z\PYZus{}DIM}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{16}
\PY{n}{H\PYZus{}DIM}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{64}

\PY{k}{class}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nc}{VAE}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Module}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf+fm}{\PYZus{}\PYZus{}init\PYZus{}\PYZus{}}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{in\PYZus{}dim}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{128}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{h\PYZus{}dim}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{H\PYZus{}DIM}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{z\PYZus{}dim}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{Z\PYZus{}DIM}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}

        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{enc} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Sequential}\PY{p}{(}
            \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Linear}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{in\PYZus{}dim}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{h\PYZus{}dim}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Softplus}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
            \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Linear}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{h\PYZus{}dim}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{h\PYZus{}dim} \PY{o}{/}\PY{o}{/} \PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Softplus}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{fc\PYZus{}mu}     \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Linear}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{h\PYZus{}dim} \PY{o}{/}\PY{o}{/} \PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{z\PYZus{}dim}\PY{p}{)}

        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Decoder: z -> x}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{dec} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Sequential}\PY{p}{(}
            \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Linear}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{z\PYZus{}dim}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{h\PYZus{}dim} \PY{o}{/}\PY{o}{/} \PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Softplus}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
            \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Linear}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{h\PYZus{}dim} \PY{o}{/}\PY{o}{/} \PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{h\PYZus{}dim}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{n}{nn}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Softplus}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}

        \PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{3.3 Level 2 --- GMM on Graph-Smoothed
Embeddings}\label{level-2-gmm-on-graph-smoothed-embeddings}

A \textbf{Gaussian Mixture Model} with \(K=15\) components fits the
joint distribution of graph-smoothed embeddings \(\tilde{H}\). Each
component \(k\) represents a latent cluster: \[
p(\tilde{h}) = \sum_{k=1}^{K} \pi_k \, \mathcal{N}(\tilde{h} \mid \mu_k, \Sigma_k),
\qquad \sum_k \pi_k = 1
\] Parameters are estimated via the \textbf{EM algorithm} (a
coordinate-ascent variational method). After fitting on \(x_{\rm orig}\)
embeddings, we compare the soft cluster assignments
\(p(k \mid \tilde{h}_{\rm orig})\) vs \(p(k \mid \tilde{h}_{\rm wm})\)
for watermarked nodes.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]

    \subsubsection{3.4 Level 3 --- Bayesian Logistic
Regression}\label{level-3-bayesian-logistic-regression}

A \textbf{Bayesian linear classifier} on \(\tilde{H}\) (PCA-reduced to
32 dims) replaces the point-estimate softmax with a full posterior over
weights \(W\): \[
W \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I), \quad
b \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I), \quad
y_v \mid W,b,\tilde{h}_v \sim \mathrm{Categorical}\bigl(\mathrm{softmax}(\tilde{h}_v W + b)\bigr)
\] Inference uses \textbf{SVI} with an \texttt{AutoDiagonalNormal}
guide, giving a mean-field Gaussian approximation
\(q(W, b) \approx p(W, b \mid \mathcal{D})\). At prediction time, we
average over 200 posterior samples to obtain a \textbf{predictive
distribution}
\(p(y \mid \tilde{h}) = \mathbb{E}_{q}[p(y \mid \tilde{h}, W, b)]\) and
compute \textbf{predictive entropy} as the uncertainty measure: \[
\mathcal{H}[y \mid \tilde{h}] = -\sum_{c=1}^{40} p(y\!=\!c \mid \tilde{h})
\log p(y\!=\!c \mid \tilde{h})
\]

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{11}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} -- Bayesian classifier definition -------------------------------------------}
\PY{k+kn}{from}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nn}{pyro}\PY{n+nn}{.}\PY{n+nn}{infer}\PY{n+nn}{.}\PY{n+nn}{autoguide}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kn}{import} \PY{n}{AutoDiagonalNormal}

\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}tr} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{tensor}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{H\PYZus{}pca\PYZus{}orig}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{train\PYZus{}mask}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{dtype}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float32}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{y\PYZus{}tr} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{train\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{long}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}te} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{tensor}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{H\PYZus{}pca\PYZus{}orig}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{test\PYZus{}mask}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{n}{dtype}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float32}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{y\PYZus{}te} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{test\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{long}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Precompute embeddings for WM nodes (x\PYZus{}orig and x\PYZus{}wm)}
\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}orig} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{tensor}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{H\PYZus{}pca\PYZus{}orig}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{dtype}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float32}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}wm}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{tensor}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{H\PYZus{}pca\PYZus{}wm}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}   \PY{n}{dtype}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float32}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{bayes\PYZus{}clf\PYZus{}model}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y\PYZus{}obs}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{W} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{pyro}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sample}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{W}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{dist}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Normal}\PY{p}{(}
            \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{zeros}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{PCA\PYZus{}DIM}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}class}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{device}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{X}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{device}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}
            \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{ones}\PY{p}{(} \PY{n}{PCA\PYZus{}DIM}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}class}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{device}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{X}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{device}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to\PYZus{}event}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{b} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{pyro}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sample}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{b}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{dist}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Normal}\PY{p}{(}

            \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{ones}\PY{p}{(} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}class}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{device}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{X}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{device}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to\PYZus{}event}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}

        \PY{n}{pyro}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sample}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{y}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{dist}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Categorical}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{logits}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{logits}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{obs}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{y\PYZus{}obs}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{bayes\PYZus{}guide} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{AutoDiagonalNormal}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{bayes\PYZus{}clf\PYZus{}model}\PY{p}{)}

\subsection{Section 4 --- Model Learning /
Inference}\label{section-4-model-learning-inference}

Both the VAE and the Bayesian classifier are trained with
\textbf{Stochastic Variational Inference (SVI)} --- maximising the ELBO
via mini-batch gradient ascent. The GMM was already fitted by EM in
Section 3.3. We monitor the per-sample ELBO throughout training as a
convergence diagnostic.

    \subsubsection{4.1 VAE Training (SVI,
mini-batch)}\label{vae-training-svi-mini-batch}

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{12}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} -- Train VAE ----------------------------------------------------------------}
\PY{n}{pyro}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{clear\PYZus{}param\PYZus{}store}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{vae} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{VAE}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{svi\PYZus{}vae}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{SVI}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{model}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{guide}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{Adam}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{\PYZob{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{lr}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{l+m+mf}{1e\PYZhy{}3}\PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{loss}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{Trace\PYZus{}ELBO}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}all\PYZus{}gpu} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{x\PYZus{}orig}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{detach}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{BATCH\PYZus{}VAE}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{512}
\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}EP\PYZus{}VAE}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{800}
\PY{n}{elbo\PYZus{}vae}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Training VAE for }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}EP\PYZus{}VAE}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{ epochs (batch=}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{BATCH\PYZus{}VAE}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{) ...}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{ep} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{range}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}EP\PYZus{}VAE}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{perm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{randperm}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{N}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{device}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{ep\PYZus{}loss} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.0}
    \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{i} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{range}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{BATCH\PYZus{}VAE}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{idx} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{perm}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{i} \PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{i} \PY{o}{+} \PY{n}{BATCH\PYZus{}VAE}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{n}{ep\PYZus{}loss} \PY{o}{+}\PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{svi\PYZus{}vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{step}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}all\PYZus{}gpu}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{idx}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{elbo\PYZus{}vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{append}\PY{p}{(}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{n}{ep\PYZus{}loss} \PY{o}{/} \PY{n}{N}\PY{p}{)}          \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} negative ELBO (loss) -> ELBO}
    \PY{k}{if} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{ep} \PY{o}{+} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{\PYZpc{}} \PY{l+m+mi}{100} \PY{o}{==} \PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{  Epoch }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{ep}\PY{o}{+}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{4d}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{/}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}EP\PYZus{}VAE}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{  ELBO/sample: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{elbo\PYZus{}vae}\PY{p}{[}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{.4f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{VAE training complete.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \subsubsection{4.2 Bayesian Classifier Training

\PY{n}{svi\PYZus{}bc} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{SVI}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{bayes\PYZus{}clf\PYZus{}model}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{bayes\PYZus{}guide}\PY{p}{,}

\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}EP\PYZus{}BC}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{1000}
\PY{n}{elbo\PYZus{}bc}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{]}
\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}tr\PYZus{}gpu} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}tr}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{;} \PY{n}{y\PYZus{}tr\PYZus{}gpu} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{y\PYZus{}tr}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Training Bayesian classifier for }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}EP\PYZus{}BC}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{ epochs ...}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{ep} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{range}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}EP\PYZus{}BC}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{loss} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{svi\PYZus{}bc}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{step}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}tr\PYZus{}gpu}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y\PYZus{}tr\PYZus{}gpu}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{elbo\PYZus{}bc}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{append}\PY{p}{(}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{n}{loss} \PY{o}{/} \PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}tr}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{if} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{ep} \PY{o}{+} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{\PYZpc{}} \PY{l+m+mi}{200} \PY{o}{==} \PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{  Epoch }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{ep}\PY{o}{+}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{4d}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{/}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}EP\PYZus{}BC}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{  ELBO/sample: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{elbo\PYZus{}bc}\PY{p}{[}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{.4f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Bayesian classifier training complete.}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}

\subsection{Section 5 --- Model
Evaluation}\label{section-5-model-evaluation}

We now interrogate each trained model with \textbf{two versions} of the
watermarked nodes: \(x_{\rm orig}\) (pre-watermark) and \(x_{\rm wm}\)
(post-watermark). The contrast between the two responses quantifies the
\textbf{probabilistic signature} of the watermark at each level of the
GCN pipeline.

    \subsubsection{5.1 Level 1 --- VAE: Feature-Space Out-of-Distribution
Detection}\label{level-1-vae-feature-space-out-of-distribution-detection}

\textbf{Metric:} per-node reconstruction MSE and KL divergence
\(D_{\rm KL}(q(z|x)\|p(z))\).

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{15}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} -- VAE evaluation -----------------------------------------------------------}
\PY{n}{vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{eval}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{with} \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{no\PYZus{}grad}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{vae\PYZus{}metrics}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}in}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{x\PYZus{}gpu} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{x\PYZus{}in}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{mu}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{lv} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{encode}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}gpu}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{x\PYZus{}hat}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{decode}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{mu}\PY{p}{)}                         \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} use posterior mean z}
        \PY{n}{mse}    \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}gpu} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{n}{x\PYZus{}hat}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{*}\PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{mean}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{dim}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}     \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} per\PYZhy{}node MSE}
        \PY{n}{kl}     \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.5} \PY{o}{*} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{mu}\PY{o}{*}\PY{o}{*}\PY{l+m+mi}{2} \PY{o}{+} \PY{n}{lv}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{exp}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{l+m+mi}{1} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{n}{lv}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{dim}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} per\PYZhy{}node KL}
        \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{mse}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{cpu}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{kl}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{cpu}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{n}{mse\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}orig}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{kl\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}orig} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{vae\PYZus{}metrics}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}orig}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} WM w/ x\PYZus{}orig}
    \PY{n}{mse\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}wm}\PY{p}{,}   \PY{n}{kl\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}wm}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{vae\PYZus{}metrics}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}wm}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}      \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} WM w/ x\PYZus{}wm}
    \PY{n}{mse\PYZus{}non}\PY{p}{,}     \PY{n}{kl\PYZus{}non}      \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{vae\PYZus{}metrics}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}orig}\PY{p}{[}\PY{o}{\PYZti{}}\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} non\PYZhy{}WM}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Latent codes for visualisation}
    \PY{n}{mu\PYZus{}all}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{\PYZus{}}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{encode}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}orig}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{mu\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}o}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{\PYZus{}}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{encode}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}orig}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{mu\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}w}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{\PYZus{}}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{vae}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{encode}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x\PYZus{}wm}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{wm\PYZus{}mask}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Reconstruction MSE:}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{  non\PYZhy{}WM  (x\PYZus{}orig) : }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{mse\PYZus{}non}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{mean}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{.5f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{ +/- }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{mse\PYZus{}non}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{std}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{.5f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{  WM      (x\PYZus{}orig) : }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{mse\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}orig}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{mean}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{.5f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{ +/- }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{mse\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}orig}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{std}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{.5f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{  WM      (x\PYZus{}wm)   : }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{mse\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}wm}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{mean}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{.5f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{ +/- }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{mse\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}wm}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{std}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s2}{.5f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\prompt{In}{incolor}{16}{\boxspacing}

    \subsubsection{5.3 Level 3 --- Bayesian Classifier: Predictive
Uncertainty}\label{level-3-bayesian-classifier-predictive-uncertainty}

We draw 200 samples from the posterior \(q(W,b)\) and average softmax
outputs to obtain the \textbf{posterior predictive distribution} for
each node. Predictive entropy \(\mathcal{H}[y|\tilde{h}]\) quantifies
how uncertain the model is.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{20}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} -- Bayesian classifier evaluation --------------------------------------------}
\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}PRED\PYZus{}SAMPLES} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{200}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{predictive\PYZus{}entropy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}in}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{n\PYZus{}samples}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}PRED\PYZus{}SAMPLES}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}Average softmax over n\PYZus{}samples posterior weight draws -> entropy.\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}gpu} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}in}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{probs\PYZus{}sum} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{zeros}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}in}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}class}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{device}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{DEVICE}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{\PYZus{}} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{range}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{n\PYZus{}samples}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Sample W, b from guide}
        \PY{n}{guide\PYZus{}trace} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{pyro}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{poutine}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{trace}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{bayes\PYZus{}guide}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{get\PYZus{}trace}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}gpu}\PY{p}{,} \PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{W\PYZus{}s} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{guide\PYZus{}trace}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{nodes}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{W}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{value}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{n}{b\PYZus{}s} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{guide\PYZus{}trace}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{nodes}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{b}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{value}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{n}{logits} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{X\PYZus{}gpu} \PY{o}{@} \PY{n}{W\PYZus{}s} \PY{o}{+} \PY{n}{b\PYZus{}s}
        \PY{n}{probs\PYZus{}sum} \PY{o}{+}\PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{torch}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{softmax}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{logits}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{dim}\PY{o}{=}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{p\PYZus{}avg} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{probs\PYZus{}sum} \PY{o}{/} \PY{n}{n\PYZus{}samples}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{detach}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{cpu}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{ent}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{p\PYZus{}avg} \PY{o}{*} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{log}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{p\PYZus{}avg} \PY{o}{+} \PY{l+m+mf}{1e\PYZhy{}12}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{axis}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{p\PYZus{}avg}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ent}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Computing predictive distributions (}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}PRED\PYZus{}SAMPLES}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{ samples each) ...}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{\PYZus{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ent\PYZus{}te\PYZus{}all}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{predictive\PYZus{}entropy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}te}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{\PYZus{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ent\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}o\PYZus{}bc}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{predictive\PYZus{}entropy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X\PYZus{}wm\PYZus{}orig}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{6.1 Three-Level Analysis:
Findings}\label{three-level-analysis-findings}

\textbf{Level 1 --- Feature space (VAE):} The VAE reconstruction error
is measurably higher for \(x_{\rm wm}\) than for \(x_{\rm orig}\) on the
same watermarked nodes (Figure 5a). This confirms that the optimised
perturbation \(\Delta x\) pushes node features out of the distribution
learned from clean data. The KL divergence
\(D_{\rm KL}(q(z|x_{\rm wm}) \| p(z))\) also increases for most WM nodes
(Figure 5b, points above the diagonal), indicating that the watermarked
features require a less probable latent code under the prior.
Nevertheless, the absolute magnitude of the shift is small ---
consistent with the design goal of imperceptibility.

\textbf{Level 2 --- Embedding space (GMM):} After 2-hop graph smoothing,
the watermark signal is partially absorbed by the neighbourhood
aggregation: \(\approx X\%\) of WM nodes change their MAP cluster
assignment when switching from \(x_{\rm orig}\) to \(x_{\rm wm}\). The
soft-assignment entropy increases for WM nodes with \(x_{\rm wm}\)
(Figure 6b), meaning the embedding perturbation moves nodes towards
cluster boundaries, increasing ambiguity in the latent structure.

\textbf{Level 3 --- Prediction space (Bayesian Classifier):} The
Bayesian classifier reveals higher predictive entropy for watermarked
nodes under \(x_{\rm wm}\) compared to \(x_{\rm orig}\) (Figure 7a--c).
The entropy change \(\Delta\mathcal{H}\) shows a positive (albeit weak)
correlation with \(\|\Delta x_v\|_2\) (Figure 7b): nodes that receive
larger perturbations tend to become more uncertain, as expected if the
watermark pushes features towards decision boundaries.

\subsubsection{6.2 Limitations}\label{limitations}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The \textbf{graph smoothing} used here (\(\tilde{A}^2 X\)) has no
  learnable parameters. A trained GCN may amplify or suppress the
  watermark signal differently.
\item
  The \textbf{GMM} is fitted on training-node embeddings only; test
  nodes may fall in slightly different regions of the embedding space.
\item
  The Bayesian classifier uses a \textbf{linear} model on PCA-compressed
  features, which may underfit the true 40-class decision boundary.
\item
  All probabilistic tools treat watermarked and non-watermarked nodes as
  \emph{independently} distributed --- ignoring the graph structure that
  couples them.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{6.3 Conclusion}\label{conclusion}

A feature-level watermark leaves a detectable probabilistic signature at
\textbf{all three levels} of a GCN pipeline. The VAE quantifies this as
an \emph{out-of-distribution} reconstruction cost; the GMM reveals
\emph{structural shifts} in embedding cluster membership; and the
Bayesian classifier exposes \emph{increased predictive uncertainty} for
affected nodes. Together, these three probabilistic lenses provide a
richer picture of watermark detectability than any single metric --- and
motivate the use of probabilistic rather than deterministic analysis for
dataset IP protection.
